The Biggest Pro-AI Super PAC Network Didn’t Really Impact the Midterms

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Leading the Future, the biggest pro-AI super PAC network, is walking away from the midterm elections with just over $20 million still in the bank and a roster of dozens of successful congressional candidates from both sides of the aisle.

At first glance, that looks like a win. The details tell another story, though. The donations went overwhelmingly to safe candidates, and the results in the small handful of riskier races that Leading the Future got involved with indicate that its plans to shape the election fell flat.

When Leading the Future entered the scene in August 2025 vowing to “oppose policies that stifle innovation," it had a war chest of around $100 million thanks to some of Silicon Valley’s biggest tech billionaires—including Andreessen Horowitz, Palantir cofounder Joe Lonsdale, and OpenAI’s Greg Brockman—and was led by major political strategists.

Designed as a “structured ecosystem,” it set a goal of having ”maximum flexibility to engage in the 2026 election cycle and beyond.” The network is made up of 501(c)(4) organizations and federal and state super PACs catering to both sides of the aisle: American Mission largely gives to Republicans, while Think Big largely gives to Democrats. Its structure and overlapping strategists quickly drew comparisons to Fairshake, a massive, well-funded pro-crypto super PAC network that dominated in the 2024 election.

“They came out guns a-blazing, saying, ‘Be afraid, we are going to spend our millions to influence not only how lawmakers legislate us but also against you in your primaries and in your reelection,’” Sacha Haworth, executive director of the Tech Oversight Project, tells WIRED. ”The purpose of that was to bully lawmakers into acquiescence, intimidate them into not sticking their necks out on AI safety or tech industry regulation at all.”

Leading the Future did not respond to requests for comment.

Late last year, Leading the Future took on Democrat Alex Bores, a former Palantir engineer and state lawmaker running in New York’s 12th congressional district on an AI safety platform, spending millions of dollars in attack ads against his primary bid.

“They fully expected to bury him, put his head on a spike, and parade it around as proof that they could,” says Haworth. Instead, Bores’ candidacy became a high-profile proxy war between competing AI philosophies, boosting his profile and platform. The pro-AI safety network Public First, which has significant financial backing from Anthropic, spent $15 million boosting Bores’ candidacy. Though Bores didn’t win, he came second in a crowded primary field to another candidate, Micah Lasher, who campaigned on tech accountability and AI safety.

Leading the Future spent about $26 million in independent expenditures on the primaries alone. But spending tapered off as it headed into the general election, as the network dropped about $10 million in total while overwhelmingly backing candidates in safe races. Its funding also dropped off, with the network reporting zero donations in the second and third quarters.

The strategy seems to have shifted around the same time that stark polling began coming back showing widespread distrust of Big Tech and cronyism in Washington, DC. (An NBC exit poll found that 56 percent of voters said AI and data centers played a “major” role in their vote.)

Backing safe candidates was, simply put, “an influence play,” says Haworth. ”They're trying to ingratiate themselves with incoming members of Congress.”

The co-founder of Public First Action says that the midterms marked a victory for AI safety. “Voters in both parties delivered the same verdict tonight,” says Brad Carson. “This is a crushing blow to the AI industry’s anti-regulatory efforts to buy a Congress that would look the other way.”

Results weren’t great in the riskier races that Leading the Future jumped into.

One of its biggest bets was on Darline Graham, the sister of the late senator Lindsey Graham and an incumbent running to serve a full term in South Carolina. Graham earned the moniker “Data Center Darline” from her Democratic opponent Annie Andrews due to her enthusiastic support of AI and data center expansion. American Mission donated more than $2 million to Graham, who ultimately lost with 46.5 percent in a state that last elected a Democrat to the Senate in 1998.

Of the other toss-up House races that Leading the Future weighed in on, almost all were in support of Republican candidates through the American Mission super PAC. The exception was Democratic Florida representative Jared Moskowitz’s reelection campaign, which it funded through Think Big. Moskowitz won by 13,000 votes against challenger Scott Singer, the former mayor of Boca Raton.

In North Carolina’s 1st congressional district, Leading the Future through American Mission backed Republican Laurie Buckhout, a retired Army colonel who served as an assistant secretary of defense working on cyber policy under President Donald Trump in 2025, in her primary. Guardrails Alliance, another player in the AI safety network, backed her opponent, Democrat Don Davis, who previously cosponsored a bill that would limit tax giveaways for data center development. Davis won.

Leading the Future also backed Aaron Flint, a retired combat veteran, conservative commentator, and former state director for Republican representative Ryan Zinke, who was running in Montana’s first congressional district. Flint conceded to his Democratic opponent Sam Forstag, a Democratic wildland firefighter who landed endorsements from major progressives including Alexandra Ocasio-Cortez and advocated for strict federal oversight of AI. (The Associated Press has yet to call the race.)

American Mission Florida, a state-level super PAC in the Leading the Future network, got $3 million in support of Republican gubernatorial candidate Byron Donalds. While both Donalds and opponent David Jolly took nuanced approaches to the AI safety issue, they diverged strongly on the issue of data center expansion, with Donalds opposing statewide bans or moratoriums on data centers.

After Tuesday night, Silicon Valley will be bracing for what’s expected to be a reckoning over the expansive power and freedom it has enjoyed thus far during Trump’s second term. Democrats have vowed to bring Big Tech bosses to Capitol Hill for hearings. Senator Ron Wyden signaled what's to come in a pithy statement on X. “Subpoenas,” he wrote.

How to Get Chocolate Chrome Nails, Hailey Bieber’s Go-To Fall Manicure

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Brown polish and chrome powder will never go out of style.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

In the unending trend cycle, there’s one fall manicure that has been trending every year for almost half a decade: chocolate chrome. “I think the secret behind the popularity of chocolate brown shades with chrome powder is how they give a familiar color palette a completely new look,” Los Angeles-based nail artist Vikki Swan says. “Chrome creates a beautiful wet-look effect with delicate shine and soft reflections.”

Our Lady of Unending Nail Inspiration, Hailey Bieber, has been a fan of brown chrome nails for years: She went with more of an iced latte shade in September 2022 (the same month she wore the cooler taupe version in the photo below) and I found evidence of her wearing a deeper chocolate glaze in 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, and, most recently, about a month ago. Bieber is known for her clean-girl aesthetic, which brown chrome definitely can fit into, but when done with a dark base polish the look can feel almost edgy—more glam rock than soft girl.

That’s all to say, chocolate chrome nails are versatile, with about a million variations that look great on every nail length and skin tone. Here, some expert tips for getting the look yourself, plus a handful of inspiration photos to bring to the salon.

“Chocolate chrome” follows the time-honored tradition of naming beauty looks after food, but these manicures are simply brown nail polish (usually gel) with chrome powder on top. Still, that basic combination can lead to some very decadent final results. “Chocolate chrome takes the richness of espresso and transforms it into the shine of molten jewelry,” says Tucson-based nail artist Max Estrada. “It's glamorous, seductive and beautifully indulgent.”

Estrada sees this year’s chocolate chromes as emblematic of a larger beauty trend. “I'm seeing a move away from apologetically understated manicures toward luxury that feels almost theatrical,” Estrada says. “Think New York or Paris in 1987 with extravagant faux-fur coats, vintage Schiaparelli, opulent dresses and a cloud of Dior Poison.”

With so many shades of brown and so many chrome powders to choose from, picking one for your own chocolate manicure can feel overwhelming. The first step is deciding what vibe you want. “Darker browns create a richer, more dramatic effect, while lighter shades give a softer, more delicate finish,” Swan says, noting that she generally prefers browns with a neutral or slightly warm undertone for this.

Whether you go with a nearly-black cocoa or a creamy caramel as your base, Estrada recommends chromes that lean more gold than silver: Think champagne, bronze, or copper (though “an airy pearl chrome adds a cooler editorial twist”). Regardless of which color you prefer, Swan says to look for a sheer chrome with a delicate pearlescent finish. “They enhance the depth of the base color without covering or overpowering the original gel polish shade,” she explains.

Brown chrome also works well in a mismatched manicure, another example of nails trending toward the theatrical. “Let's be extravagant!” Estrada says. “Mix chocolate chrome with black-cherry velvet cat-eye, deep oxblood French tips, tortoiseshell, baroque gold detailing, sculpted accents and tiny crystals.” Swan also loves brown chrome paired with tortoiseshell and gold. “The key is to stay within a cohesive color palette so the different designs complement each other without making the manicure look too busy,” she says.

While it is possible to do chrome over regular polish, the look is more commonly created with a gel set, since the base color needs to be totally dry before you rub the chrome on top. “I recommend applying chrome powder after curing, while the top coat is still warm,” Swan says. “After application, it’s important to seal the chrome thoroughly with a finishing top coat to minimize the risk of chipping and ensure long-lasting results.”

Both Swan also emphasizes the importance of having a smooth base under the chrome, which requires thorough nail preparation. “Chrome powder can highlight even the smallest imperfections,” she says. “Proper nail architecture and proportions are essential, along with a high-quality top coat that creates a perfectly smooth surface.”

While chocolate chrome can work on any nail length, Estrada thinks they’re especially luxurious on longer tips. “I love these colors on a long structured coffin, exaggerated almond or beautifully sharp stiletto,” he says, noting that long nails make the decadent finish even more striking. “This winter, I want excess, glamour and artistry. Too much is precisely the point!”

Kara McGrath is a contributing beauty editor at Cosmopolitan with over 15 years of experience covering the beauty space and an at-home nail art enthusiast. Both jobs include browsing and saving countless manicure ideas for research, plus chatting with professional artists for insight into current nail trends.

Apple cuts engineers’ daily AI budgets as it reins in spending

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Some engineers' daily cap fell from $300 to $200, and some other teams could drop to $25.

The Apple logo on the lid of a silver MacBook Pro.

Apple cut the daily AI spending cap for some engineers from $300 to $200 last month and plans further cuts, Bloomberg reports. It comes as CEO John Ternus pushes for a leaner Apple, while the company’s R&D costs rose 32% on AI and infrastructure.

Apple has cut how much some engineers can spend on AI models each day, from $300 to $200, Bloomberg reported on Wednesday, citing people familiar with the change. It plans more cuts that could take some engineering teams down to $100 a day.

Many staff outside engineering already have a $100 daily cap, and Apple has considered lowering that to $25 for some teams. Employees who need more can ask senior leaders for an exemption. Apple declined to comment.

The budgets cover tokens, the units of data that AI models process. Writing and debugging code can use a lot of them. Apple staff can use Anthropic’s Claude, OpenAI’s ChatGPT and Google’s Gemini, and some teams are being told to switch to cheaper models or ones that run on the device.

Reactions inside Apple are mixed, according to Bloomberg. Some staff say few people used their full $300 allowance. Others see the cuts as a sign of wider cost cutting and worry about layoffs, after Apple cut over 200 jobs in August.

Since becoming chief executive on 1 September, John Ternus has pushed Apple to run faster and leaner, and has cut layers of management. Some divisions have also hired outside consultants to review how efficiently they work.

Apple’s overall spending is still rising. Research and development costs rose 32% in its 2026 fiscal year on higher infrastructure and AI costs, the company said in its annual report this week. Operating expenses rose 22% to $75.5B.

Apple is not alone. Microsoft set division-level AI budgets in July, Tesla caps staff at $200 a week and Uber limits each AI coding tool to $1,500 a month. Accenture has gone further, capping Claude at $29 a month.

Across the industry, firms are capping staff AI use as their bills grow.

Defiant Ritz-Carlton Singapore Joining Marriott Bonvoy… Finally!

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The world’s only Ritz-Carlton that doesn’t participate in the Marriott Bonvoy program will finally be joining, which seems long overdue. I can’t imagine the hotel will be particularly generous, but we’ll see!

The 608-key Ritz-Carlton Millenia Singapore will be joining the Marriott Bonvoy program as of December 1, 2026. So as of that date, Bonvoy members will be able to earn and redeem points, and take advantage of Bonvoy elite perks, when staying at the hotel (admittedly Bonvoy elite perks at Ritz-Carlton properties are fairly limited, as breakfast isn’t a benefit for any elite tier).

As I covered in late 2025, this property is the only Ritz-Carlton in the world that doesn’t participate in the Bonvoy program. The backstory here is that the hotel opened back in 1996, and at the time, Ritz-Carlton didn’t have any sort of a loyalty program that awarded points.

In 2010, the Ritz-Carlton Rewards program started awarding points for hotel stays, as a parallel program to Marriott Rewards. The ability to earn and redeem points came at a cost to individual hotels, but it was intended to boost occupancy and average daily rates.

Initially, hotels had the option of whether they wanted to opt in or out of the program. Most hotels bought into the concept, though the Ritz-Carlton Osaka and Ritz-Carlton Singapore were the two hotels that opted out. The Ritz-Carlton Osaka eventually decided to participate, leaving the Ritz-Carlton Singapore as the only hotel that’s not participating.

Ultimately when the Marriott Bonvoy program formed as the combination of Marriott Rewards and Starwood Preferred Guest, the hotel could still stick to its legacy contract of not having to participate in the program. Clearly the owners of the hotel have now had a change of heart… or something!

Obviously we don’t have any sort of an official explanation for the reason that this Ritz-Carlton property is finally backtracking. I can’t imagine that Marriott gave the owners an ultimatum, given that Marriott is known for letting hotel owners get away with anything, as long as they get paid.

My guess is that the competitive realities just finally caught up with this hotel — Singapore is an increasingly competitive hotel market, this is a massive hotel with 600+ rooms to fill every night, and the hotel is also a bit past its prime.

The truth is that loyalty programs exist for a reason, as they’re a great tool for getting heads into beds. Obviously the hotel will be on the hook for some additional loyalty fees, but I imagine this will make the hotel more appealing to people who otherwise wouldn’t have considered it.

I also have to imagine that even though the hotel did a decent job communicating its lack of participation in Marriott Bonvoy, I still suspect many guests didn’t realize this, and ended up being disappointed.

As of December 1, 2026, the Ritz-Carlton Singapore will be joining the Marriott Bonvoy program. This is the only Ritz-Carlton hotel in the world that hasn’t participated in Bonvoy, so this is great to see.

The backstory is that the hotel opened before Ritz-Carlton had a loyalty program, and then when a program was finally formed, participation was optional. Literally every other Ritz-Carlton in the world saw value in this… except this one, apparently.

Fortunately they’ve now come to their senses, or something, so it’s great to see that this will be changing.

What do you make of the Ritz-Carlton Singapore joining Bonvoy?

Of Mics and Men: After MAGA Meltdown, the Manosphere Winds Down

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Two years ago, on the night of the 2024 presidential election, Ultimate Fighting Championship CEO Dana White took to the podium at Donald Trump’s Palm Beach victory party and rattled off a list of thank-yous to some rowdy podcasters and YouTube pranksters: the NELK Boys, Adin Ross, Theo Von, Bussin’ With the Boys, and “last but not least, the mighty and powerful Joe Rogan.” Just months before, these names were seldom mentioned in the mainstream press, but now they were recognized on a national stage as having powered Trump’s return to office by hosting him on their platforms and revealing his more personable side. The victory that night seemed to go well beyond the electoral conquest; one of the defining cultural legacies of the 2024 election cycle is the imprecise but vivid concept of the manosphere.

By 2026, the novelty of Trump and Von joking about the potency of cocaine, as they did in one of the signature moments of the 2024 campaign, has worn off. The president’s approval rankings tanked despite his enduring presence as a pop culture figure. Rogan endorsed Trump in the final days of that 2024 race, but he recently described Trump’s “really crazy” 1,300-word Truth Social tirade against Bill Maher as having the ring of a “girl who got dumped.” He has roundly criticized Trump’s handling of the Epstein Files, a signature issue for the podcast class, and described the Iran war as a “betrayal.” If the end of MAGA is in sight amid Tuesday’s blue tsunami, so is the notion that Republicans had captured the coveted, low-propensity demographic of young men: The New York Times estimated that Democrats won 18-to-29-year-old men by 24 points on Tuesday, a 38-point swing from Trump’s 2024 victory among them. Democrats are content creators too now, and the most notable political appearance on The Joe Rogan Experience over the past couple of years was James Talarico’s. “You need to run for president,” Rogan told Texas’s new senator-elect.

And so when C-SPAN made the somewhat mind-boggling announcement that the staid public-affairs network would simulcast election night coverage hosted by Patrick Bet-David, a Florida-based podcaster known for his interviews with Andrew Tate and Nick Fuentes, it was less of a capitulation to the so-called manosphere than an emblem of the modern media free-for-all. Of the online personalities loosely associated with Rogan’s milieu, Bet-David’s backstory is the most colorful. A refugee from Iran, he joined the Army upon arriving in the United States; he has said he worked for a time as a bodyguard to a high-level Los Angeles cocaine dealer. Bet-David now operates a media company called Valuetainment, formed after he made his riches with an insurance company that has been widely described as a multi-level marketing operation. After interviewing the leading looksmaxxing personality, Clavicular, last week, he was now responsible for providing C-SPAN’s conservative perspective. A spokesperson for the network described the programming to me as an effort to achieve “a marriage of traditional media and new media.”

“In the 2024 election, we invited Pete Buttigieg, God knows how many times, never showed up,” Bet-David told me in the lead-up to the evening. “Pete Buttigieg was here last week. We did a two-hour podcast together right before the midterms. And so the market is realizing that’s the direction you got to go.”

As he entertained a characteristically ragtag group of political-for-the-night personalities, Bet-David sat at his decision desk in a pinstripe suit with cartoonishly wide peak lapels. There were the young conservative provocateurs CJ Pearson and Nick Shirley, and the Groyper-adjacent, recent Florida gubernatorial candidate James Fishback, who described the night as a “really tough election for my party.”

Bet-David joked to Michael Franzese that he knows about gas prices—before refashioning himself as a political commentator, the former Colombo crime family capo ran a multi-million-dollar gasoline tax bootlegging scheme in the 1980s. In voicing his opposition to the war in Iran, Franzese said, “We had a saying on the street, the best way to defeat your enemies is to make them your friends.” He bemoaned how Trump “makes everything all about him.”

Other guests included Ann Coulter, Piers Morgan, Roger Stone, Bill O’Reilly, and Cenk Uygur, whose Young Turks show was simulcasting on another C-SPAN channel. For some reason, Jackass’s Steve-O appeared toward the end of the night. He pointed out to Bet-David that Marco Rubio is “stronger for you guys” than JD Vance.

Two years ago, I watched a similar scene unfold in an airplane hangar in Fort Lauderdale. It was, as Bet-David and I agreed on Tuesday, a wild night: He was hosting an election night party at Valuetainment’s headquarters at the peak of the manosphere chatter and at the onset of what seemed to so many to be the era of MAGA cultural domination. Men in tightly tapered polyester pants hopped out of Ferraris and Teslas to watch a crowd of UFC fighters, rappers, former mobsters, and Candace Owens wax poetic about how the age of Jennifer Lopez and Beyoncé, who had appeared alongside Kamala Harris in previous weeks, was over. In this new era of masculinity, there was no time anymore for liberal Hollywood political pieties: Mark Zuckerberg had just started wearing a gold chain and describing Trump’s fist pump after the attempted assassination as “one of the most badass things I’ve ever seen in my life.”

And the vibe shift, as it could only be called, was shocking for a period. There was a moment, for instance, in the heady aftermath of the 2024 election when Von attended an Oscars week poker night hosted by Timothée Chalamet at the Chateau Marmont. (The actor had recently appeared on his show.) Just weeks later, Von was at a UFC fight where White warmly embraced the Tate brothers. The entire category of celebrity appeared to be flattening in real time.

But to those on the inside of the podcaster-comedian boom, another swing of the pendulum already seemed to be coming. Andrew Schulz, a frequent Rogan guest, conducted one of Trump’s trademark podcast interviews in the summer of 2024. “After the Trump thing, I wasn’t like, ‘We got it now,’” he told me the following year. “After the Trump thing, I was like, ‘We just interviewed the president of the United States, what the fuck?’” He thought that the deafening manosphere discourse might indicate the presence of a cultural bubble. “I just think that we are what people are listening to now,” he said, “but that could change tomorrow.”

Alex Bruesewitz, a 29-year-old Trump adviser, was largely credited with orchestrating the candidate’s podcast tour in 2024. A social media operative who had built connections with big-name celebrities, influencers, and podcasters in the lead-up to the presidential election—he had brokered Trump’s alliance with Nicki Minaj and recently flew on Air Force One with Mike Tyson—Bruesewitz told me on Wednesday morning that he had soured on the Republican party.

“I consider myself more of a Trump voter than a Republican voter,” he said. “If the party moves in the direction of people like Ted Cruz, for example, in 2028, there's going to be a lot of people like myself…who will not participate in the Republican elections moving forward.”

In the midterms cycle, Bruesewitz said, the podcasting world “wasn’t there for us to engage.” The likes of Von and Schulz have echoed Rogan’s criticisms of the Iran war and how the Trump administration managed the release of the Epstein files.

“I don’t think that any of them were ever team Trump,” he said. “We simply just had conversations with them in 2024. The post-election reaction, saying that they’re part of this right-wing manosphere, I think that actually had an effect on how they covered us during our first two years. I think that they didn’t want to be labeled right-wing, and so they evened out a bit.”

Another reason, Bruesewitz supposed, for the end of Republican podcasting dominance and the blue tsunami: “Republican candidates were really lame and they weren’t interesting people…there was a little bit of an expectation that these candidates could go communicate with low-propensity voters with podcasting again, but I pushed back on their teams. I was like, Look, there’s zero percent chance that any of these podcasters are going to want to have on your boring candidates.”

By the end of the night on Tuesday, Bet-David and Steve-O were making plans to hang out before the stuntman left town. All of the guests on the C-SPAN–Bet-David collaboration seemed likely to keep talking about politics online, but it would be difficult to assign any of them a specific party agenda. In the airplane hangar two years prior, Bet-David had ended the night with what felt like a seismic exhortation, telling a raucous crowd, “For the rest of our lives we will share this night together.” On Tuesday, he was more practical as he signed off for the evening.

Self-Gifting Ideas For Moms: 28 Holiday Gifts To Treat Yourself

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Oh, we’re stuffing our own stockings again? Well, they’re gonna be filled with things we love, gosh damn it.

Every December, moms pull off a small miracle, and we do it mostly on our own. The presents get bought, wrapped, and tucked under the tree with tender care. The stockings get stuffed, the Elf gets Shelf’ed, the fridge and pantry get filled with foods everyone in the family (even your little picky eater) will enjoy. And nearly every December, moms end up with an empty stocking or whatever drugstore special got picked up last-minute because, oops, Mom doesn’t have anything under the tree again!

If your family actually remembers to toss a present or two under the tree or in the stocking for you, you’ve probably grown accustomed to getting the same gift, or some variation of it, every year. A “World’s Best Mom” mug. A bath bomb. You know the type. It’s fine. Really. We know it’s the thought that counts! But we also know that moms spend the entire holiday season thinking about everyone else’s wish lists, and nobody’s thinking very hard about ours.

That’s exactly why we here at Scary Mommy are big fans of taking matters into our own hands. Buy these gifts for yourself, stuff your own stocking, use ‘em to drop some very unsubtle hints on your partner’s phone, or forward this list straight to your friends and family. Because you, dear friend, deserve gifts you’ll genuinely love... and not another GD bath bomb (unless that’s your thing).

Every time I put these on the bed, my husband and I waller around like piggies in mud. They're so soft! You must waller, smiling with your eyes shut, to really enjoy them. I adore everything about these sheets and am jonesing to replace some of our old cheap sheet sets with more. They feel breathable, don't make my night sweats worse like cheap sheets do, and they actually STAY on our mattress. The entire Golden Beryl Collection is hormone-conscious, Leaping Bunny certified, pregnancy-safe, and free from parabens, phthalates, sulfates, and synthetic fragrances, which in clean beauty is genuinely rare. — Katie Garrity, Senior Editor, News & Social

Electric hypercar wrecks at 280 mph during test, driver returns for even faster run

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A nearly 3,000-horsepower electric hypercar crashed during a high-speed test while attempting to reach 280 mph, but its driver escaped without serious injuries before the manufacturer returned for an even faster run.

Chinese automaker BYD shared footage of its Yangwang U9 Xtreme crashing into a guardrail during testing, leaving much of the vehicle destroyed while its carbon-fiber cabin remained intact.

The incident comes as BYD prepares to push the electric hypercar beyond its previous top speed of 308 mph, with the company targeting a new record of more than 310 mph.

According to Electrek, the Yangwang U9 Xtreme was undergoing a series of high-speed tests when the accident occurred.

During its first run, the vehicle exceeded 217 mph before reaching more than 261 mph on its second attempt. The third run was intended to push the car beyond 280 mph, but it ended when the hypercar lost control and slammed into a guardrail.

Footage shared by BYD shows the vehicle striking the barrier before spinning across the track, with debris scattering across the road. The front and rear sections sustained extensive damage, although the central carbon-fiber passenger compartment remained intact.

Despite the crash, the driver escaped without serious injuries. BYD confirmed that the driver underwent a comprehensive medical examination at a hospital, where doctors determined they were in good health.

The manufacturer subsequently resumed testing, with another run reaching 284.5 mph, exceeding the speed targeted during the earlier crash. Yangwang representative Jiang Gong said there was still room for improvement and expressed confidence that the vehicle could eventually surpass 310 mph.

The Yangwang U9 Xtreme is powered by four electric motors producing nearly 3,000 horsepower. It also features a 1,200-volt electrical system and motors capable of reaching 30,000 rpm.

Electric hypercar sets new top speed record with 308.4 mph run

New Chinese bullet train shatters world record by going 497 mph in 5.3 seconds

Chinese ‘Superman’ robot beats Usain Bolt’s top speed – before crashing into a wall

In September 2025, the hypercar reached 308.3 mph during testing in Germany, establishing a new benchmark for production-car top speed.

BYD is now preparing another attempt to exceed 310 mph, with Dubai previously identified as the planned location for a record run in November 2026. However, the company has not confirmed a final date for the attempt.

The automaker ended its crash footage with a message indicating that it intends to continue pursuing higher speeds.

“The ultimate pursuit has no end; the summit will eventually be reached,” BYD said in a statement translated by Electrek.

‘They deserve it’ – Kostas Tsimikas admits familiar failing cost Liverpool in Istanbul

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Kostas Tsimikas admitted that Liverpool got what they deserved in their 3-2 defeat to Fenerbahce in Istanbul on Wednesday.

The Reds were in front twice at the Sukru Saracoglu Stadium but still contrived to come away with nothing against Dirk Kuyt’s side, who claimed all three points thanks to Vedat Muriqi’s 79th-minute header.

Jeremy Jacquet was criticised for turning his back on Mason Greenwood’s free kick for the first equaliser, and Alisson Becker was culpable of poor positioning for Archie Brown’s goal to make it 2-2.

Speaking to UEFA.com after the match, Tsimikas magnanimously conceded that Fenerbahce were full value for their win and lamented Liverpool’s defending over the goals that they shipped.

The Greek defender said (via BBC Sport): “Congratulations to Fenerbahce, they deserve it. We didn’t play how we had to play. We didn’t defend after the goal we conceded. After three or four minutes, automatically we conceded another goal.

“I think we have to mark better if we want to win games. We didn’t play very good and that’s why we lost.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

It sums up how careless Liverpool were this evening that they were in the lead twice and occupied a winning position for only eight minutes against Fenerbahce, with the goals from Trey Nyoni and Alexander Isak cancelled out all too quickly.

The manner of all three goals that the home side scored will enrage Iraola – it wasn’t as though the Istanbul outfit had to produce moments of magic to inspire their way to victory over the Reds.

Tsimikas himself was far from convincing, enduring a ‘tough night’ in the words of Liverpool Echo reporter Paul Gorst as he struggled to contain the threat of Greenwood, and he ended up being substituted for Milos Kerkez with 15 minutes remaining.

Liverpool have now conceded 10 goals in their four Champions League matches, shipping three for the third European fixture in a row, a record which’ll surely concern the head coach at this phase of the competition.

The debrief from this game will not be a pleasant one, and a much better defensive performance will be required if the Reds are to return to winning ways against Crystal Palace on Sunday.

Arkane union says closing the studio "would probably be more expensive" for Xbox than simply finishing Marvel's Blade

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

"Microsoft based its decision only on stone cold and inhuman logic."

Xbox would probably spend more money shutting down Arkane Lyon than it would trying to finish and release Marvel's Blade, according to a union representing workers from the beloved imsim studio.

In a statement posted to the STJV (Syndicat des travailleureuses du jeu vidéo) website, the French union claimed that development on Marvel's Blade is "going fine" and is shaping up to be on par with Arkane's widely acclaimed gameography. By choosing to part ways with and potentially close the famed studio, the union says Microsoft is "sabotaging the game it funded."

After Xbox's sweeping job cuts and divestments from several first-party studios, Arkane was recently given until December to find a new buyer and ensure its future. Ubisoft Entertainment (Assassin's Creed, Rainbow Six) is reportedly one of the companies interested in buying the studio behind Dishonored, Prey and the upcoming superhero adaptation, though nothing has been finalised yet.

The union also blamed many of the studio's problems on Microsoft - a hiring freeze that lasted more than a year supposedly inflated the game's budget and caused additional internal delays. "Microsoft now use those same delay and additional costs as a reason to close the studio," the statement said.

"Even this closure itself is done against any financial logic, as closing Arkane today would probably be more expensive than finishing the game," it continued. "The truth is that Microsoft based its decision only on stone cold and inhuman logic, in which our lives, projects and dreams are voided, so we can better serve Microsoft's short term financial goals[...] The decision to close Arkane is solely the brutal application of a never-ending search for maximum profit optimization. Arkane is profitable. Just not profitable for Microsoft."

The union's list of demands now asks for employees to be able to have a say in choosing a potential buyer, as well as protections against any layoffs that might happen in the next few years. Should acquisition talks fail, the union claimed that the resources in Microsoft's redundancy package are "outrageously low" - so it's also demanding a redundancy payment of €200,000 per employee and further support.

Arkane's not the only developer reeling from Xbox's cuts. Double Fine (Psychonauts), Compulsion Games (South of Midnight) and Undead Labs (State of Decay) all managed to buy their independence, but had to lay off some staff as a result. It's not looking good for Hellblade developer Ninja Theory, either.

Ubisoft and at least two other companies are interested in buying Arkane, according to new report
